# AI Media Scout — 2026-10-18

Today’s edition contains 42 full guides and 21 additional screened discoveries, adding 40 distinct tools and completing 23 earlier pending guides. Highlights include neural brushstroke painting, local music and dubbing, interactive video rendering, audio codecs and editable CAD. All 450 repository queries and 17 model-task queries ran across 60 fields; 109 repository queries reached result bounds. All eight configured web ecosystems were researched. Unavailable primary pages and three missing watchlist repositories are disclosed below. The complete catalog received a metadata scan followed by bounded manual checks; unreviewed matches remain private. Every selected tool has a complete software-license review. Model, asset and service terms remain separate. These are documentation reviews, not hands-on tests or claims of new launches; missing quality evidence stays explicit.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Read visual-style, restoration and perceptual-image research pages. New brushstroke guides distinguish learned painting from conventional palette math; the palette integration clearly identifies the separate image generator. [Source 1](https://curryjung.github.io/VisualStylePrompt/) [Source 2](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) |
| Video, animation & film | Read WanMove and OpenFilm primary descriptions and completed a Nuzky editing guide. Preview playback, an editable timeline and a successful export are separate checks; no renderer was run. Newly screened Framefields and openvidstudio expose concrete agent-driven rendering/capture workflows; their external model and renderer terms remain separate. [Source 1](https://wan-move.github.io/) [Source 2](https://animationai.github.io/AnimateAnything/) |
| Audio, music & voice | Read Neutone host/SDK information and GitLab audio-model integrations. Completed music-production, stem-separation and agent-authored sound guides, distinguishing neural synthesis from conventional instrument rendering. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/project/neutone_sdk/) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | Read neural surface/dynamics and scene-representation research. Completed GaussianGPT scene-generation and splat-segment object-isolation guides, with CUDA and model/data limits explicit. [Source 1](https://open3dvlab.github.io/NeuRodin/) [Source 2](https://xjay18.github.io/NeuMA_page/) [Source 3](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Browser tools & web media | Read NodeTool and OpenFilm contexts and reviewed OpenViz, Lightheart and OpenCalque. Browser access does not establish offline generation or native GPU support; script-shell pages supplied no runtime evidence. [Source 1](https://nodetool.ai/) [Source 2](https://image.solusikoding.com) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | Read Meta immersive-web and Google XR Blocks prototyping materials. Agent-created XR scenes still require actual device and interaction testing; no headset or deployment was tested. [Source 1](https://developers.meta.com/vr/blog/accelerate-vr-development-with-ai-and-immersive-web-sdk/) [Source 2](https://research.google/blog/vibe-coding-xr-accelerating-ai-xr-prototyping-with-xr-blocks-and-gemini/) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Read neural collage and compression research, then completed learned painting and interactive style-exploration guides. Completed Roqer agent editing and sound workflows while documenting proprietary Roblox and separate model/service dependencies. A procedural graph, model-generated texture and trained material estimator remain distinct workflows. Event sensors and lensless masks are physical requirements for acquisition, not implied by notebook access. [Source 1](https://rpg.ifi.uzh.ch/E2VID.html) [Source 2](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) |

| AI landscape, garden and environmental design | Read GardenDesigner’s agent-generated scene context. No new eligible tool with independently verified planting or site-design results was established in this bounded pass. [Source 1](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Neural mosaic and stained-glass media | Read neural collage representations and reviewed stroke/halftone/palette-adjacent tools. Conventional mosaic optimization alone is not included as AI. [Source 1](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI optical illusions and anamorphic media | Read Visual Anagrams, Factorized Diffusion and Diffusion Illusions primary pages. Previously profiled unchanged tools are not presented again as new discoveries. [Source 1](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/factorized_diffusion/) [Source 3](https://diffusionillusions.com/) |
| AI relief engraving and learned halftoning | Read inverse-halftoning research and the current Deep Halftoning README. Reconstructed tonal detail is not laser/process safety or print fidelity, and its unreproducible paper metrics remain explicit. [Source 1](https://houxianxu.github.io/project/inverse-halftoning) [Source 2](https://github.com/Nikronic/Deep-Halftoning) |
| Neural brushstroke painting and drawn media | New planned field: read Compositional Neural Painter and Stylized Neural Painting research, then reviewed MambaPainter, LearningToPaint and PaintTransformer software. Missing or conflicting licenses were held; available licensed implementations receive guides. A final query-result pass also found neural-painter notebooks, a watercolor GAN and a coordinate-to-color learning experiment; the neural-painter notebooks now have a complete guide, while watercolor and legacy coordinate-to-color code remain pending. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.03504) [Source 2](https://jiupinjia.github.io/neuralpainter/) [Source 3](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter) |
| AI color direction and palette-based media | New planned field: read NeuralPreset’s paper and inspected palette-conditioning tools. NeuralPreset lacks a retrieved software license; the included palette workflow distinguishes conventional color processing from its separate AI generator. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13511) [Source 2](https://github.com/SurrealByDesign/ComfyUI-Krea-Palette-Tools) |

## Search and review counts

60 creative fields; 450/450 repository queries attempted; 17/17 model-task queries attempted. 25573 distinct source candidates and 795 model leads. 42 detailed profiles and 21 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 109 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Read the first 50 diffusion-search results and followed AudioSR, LongCat AudioDiT TTS and Foundation-1 node repositories into current README/license evidence. Two MIT audio integrations qualify for screening. The Foundation-1 wrapper LICENSE lookup failed and its README describes restricted model terms, so it is held for software-license clarification. Mirrors are not counted as distinct upstream tools. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=diffusion&simple=true&per_page=50) [Source](https://gitlab.com/drbaph/ComfyUI-AudioSR) [Source](https://gitlab.com/drbaph/ComfyUI-LongCat-AudioDIT-TTS)
- **codeberg** · searched: The diffusion API request timed out; a supplemental neural query succeeded and returned 50 bounded results. Reviewed descriptions include neural colorization, archaeological image classification, speech bindings and many generic ML repositories or mirrors. No additional distinct tool received a complete current license review in this pass. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=neural&limit=50)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Read the neural project search and supplemental diffusion/audio pages. Generic ML and conventional audio entries are not automatically creative AI tools. This bounded pass did not establish another newly licensed, distinct application. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=neural) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=audio)
- **packages** · searched: Read current PyPI JSON metadata for neutone-sdk 1.5.2 and the SDAI F-Droid listing. The PyPI HTML project page returned a challenge, so the successful JSON endpoint is distinguished from that failed page; registry license labels are not complete software-license reviews. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/neutone-sdk/json) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.shifthackz.aisdv1.app.foss/)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Read Neutone and QGIS primary pages, ComfyUI audio nodes and palette workflow documentation. Host requirements, external weights and optional cloud generation are separate from plugin source terms. [Source](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/ai_agent/) [Source](https://gitlab.com/drbaph/ComfyUI-LongCat-AudioDIT-TTS)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary-page reads supplement all effective fields, including neural brushstrokes and color direction. SyncLight TLS failure and title-only/script-shell results from several sites remain explicit limitations. [Source](https://jiupinjia.github.io/neuralpainter/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13511) [Source](https://materialapprentice.github.io/)
- **research-code** · searched: Followed painting, relighting, neural compression, lensless audio and caustics papers into repository code and full software licenses. An MIT file in an otherwise empty NeRC repository is not a released implementation; conflicting painting terms are held. [Source](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter) [Source](https://github.com/Huage001/PaintTransformer) [Source](https://github.com/JiaxiongQ/NeRC)
- **international** · searched: Read Turkish microtonal OMR, Chinese/English creative tool documentation and the German INTEX textile case study. Language and popularity are not quality verdicts; model/data rights and platform limits remain explicit. [Source](https://github.com/EmreDikimen/Turkish_note_to_solfeggio_converter) [Source](https://github.com/riorgord/SlimeMold-Artist-Explorer) [Source](https://www.green-ai-hub.de/pilotprojekte/pilotprojekt-intex-gmbh)

## AI Music Studio

Audio, music & voice · AI music notation and score recovery

First complete guide, expanding an earlier screened library entry. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-18; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Compose songs, keep editable generation settings, and turn audio into a correctable multi-instrument score.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-18. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README describes portable-build workflows and measured CPU examples; a clone does not include the runnable package or models. [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Build the unrestricted source edition, then prepare the separately documented music and transcription runtimes. Node builds alone do not enable inference. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** GPL-3.0-only original code. ACE-Step, YourMT3, Whisper and optional voice/re-lyric models retain separate terms; model weights and portable binaries are absent from Git. Local compute after initial downloads; no online music-generation API required. [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Automatic notes and lyrics need correction. Optional voice conversion and local re-lyric modules are experimental and are not the workflow assessed here.

### How it uses AI

Combines ACE-Step/acestep.cpp music generation with YourMT3 transcription and Whisper lyric alignment in a Chinese-language local workspace. [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)

### Introduction

Combines ACE-Step/acestep.cpp music generation with YourMT3 transcription and Whisper lyric alignment in a Chinese-language local workspace. ### What it is good for

Compose songs, keep editable generation settings, and turn audio into a correctable multi-instrument score. ### Demo & examples

The README describes portable-build workflows and measured CPU examples; a clone does not include the runnable package or models. [Source 1](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)

### Install

Build the unrestricted source edition, then prepare the separately documented music and transcription runtimes. Node builds alone do not enable inference. [Source 1](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build the unrestricted source edition, then prepare the separately documented music and transcription runtimes. Node builds alone do not enable inference.

```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
npm run build:ui
```


```sh
pwsh -File build.ps1 -Edition Unrestricted -Version 1.3.2 -OutputDirectory data/source-build
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)

1. Prepare the engine and models using SOURCE-BUILD.md.
2. Write a style and lyrics; start with the 30-second preview.
3. Listen, save audio and parameters, then correct any transcribed score before exporting MusicXML, MIDI or SVG.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Alan-997/local-music/blob/main/docs/CROSS-PLATFORM.md)

- **Hardware:** CPU inference; developer recommends 32 GB RAM. Portable components about 5.8 GiB plus at least 2 GB work space. No discrete GPU required for the documented CPU route.
- **Software:** Node 22–24 (project pin 24.9.0), PowerShell and the Windows .NET C# compiler for source builds; packaged Windows runtime requires .NET Framework 4.7.2+ and a browser.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10 1809+/11 x64 is the main runtime. Mac ARM/Intel and Linux x64/ARM64 build routes exist; complete native Mac/Linux generation acceptance is still pending.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** GPL-3.0-only original code. ACE-Step, YourMT3, Whisper and optional voice/re-lyric models retain separate terms; model weights and portable binaries are absent from Git.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0-only original code. ACE-Step, YourMT3, Whisper and optional voice/re-lyric models retain separate terms; model weights and portable binaries are absent from Git.
- **Cost:** Local compute after initial downloads; no online music-generation API required.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. ## Sonora Studio

Audio, music & voice · Browser tools & web media

First complete guide, expanding an earlier screened library entry. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-18; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Iterate song prompts and lyrics, organize versions, and compare local or explicitly configured rented-GPU generation.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-18. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Published screenshots use demo tracks; they do not prove real generated-song quality. [Source](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Node, FFmpeg and current ComfyUI first. Start Sonora, connect the backend and use its model-file check and confirmed downloader. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT app; ACE-Step weights and optional separator/assistant models retain their own licenses. Local generation needs no AI API key. Optional OpenAI helpers, RunPod and Vast.ai use separately billed accounts. [Source](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The quick test establishes a pipeline run, not musical quality. Remote instances must be managed separately and may keep accruing charges after disconnect.

### How it uses AI

A browser music workspace that submits ACE-Step 1.5 generation to a separately managed ComfyUI installation, with prompt, library, stem and audio-analysis tools. [Source](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A browser music workspace that submits ACE-Step 1.5 generation to a separately managed ComfyUI installation, with prompt, library, stem and audio-analysis tools. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Iterate song prompts and lyrics, organize versions, and compare local or explicitly configured rented-GPU generation. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

Published screenshots use demo tracks; they do not prove real generated-song quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Node, FFmpeg and current ComfyUI first. Start Sonora, connect the backend and use its model-file check and confirmed downloader. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Node, FFmpeg and current ComfyUI first. Start Sonora, connect the backend and use its model-file check and confirmed downloader.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio.git
```


```sh
cd sonora-studio
```


```sh
npm start
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open localhost:3210 and enter the ComfyUI URL and actual model directory.
2. Download the checked model files and run the hidden 10-second pipeline test.
3. Write style and lyrics, create a track, listen and save useful versions.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** README cites 12 GB VRAM minimum with offloading, 16+ GB preferred, and 22 GB free disk (30 preferred); these are borrowed model estimates, not Sonora measurements. System RAM is not specified.
- **Software:** Node 22+, ComfyUI with ACE-Step nodes, FFmpeg; optional Python separator dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Documented local generation: Windows 10/11 or Linux with NVIDIA. Mac local inference support is not established; a browser alone does not establish it.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT app; ACE-Step weights and optional separator/assistant models retain their own licenses.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT app; ACE-Step weights and optional separator/assistant models retain their own licenses.
- **Cost:** Local generation needs no AI API key. Optional OpenAI helpers, RunPod and Vast.ai use separately billed accounts.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The quick test establishes a pipeline run, not musical quality. Remote instances must be managed separately and may keep accruing charges after disconnect. [Source 1](https://github.com/barbarabax/sonora-studio/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Duration fitting is not lip sync; overlaps are allowed by default. Separation and speaker assignment can fail with music or concurrent dialogue.

### How it uses AI

A local dialogue-dubbing application combining speech recognition, diarization, translation, reference-voice synthesis and editable segment timing. [Source](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Introduction

A local dialogue-dubbing application combining speech recognition, diarization, translation, reference-voice synthesis and editable segment timing. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### What it is good for

Create and revise multilingual dubbing, then export a video or a dialogue/background/SRT editing package. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository documents a 0.1.0 preview and dialogue studio. Windows is the most exercised platform; other packages are for testing. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

### Install

Choose the official ZIP, AppImage or Apple Silicon DMG and verify its release checksum. The package includes runtimes; first-run setup downloads about 8 GiB of pinned models. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the official ZIP, AppImage or Apple Silicon DMG and verify its release checksum. The package includes runtimes; first-run setup downloads about 8 GiB of pinned models.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Import a short video and choose its dialogue track.
2. Correct transcript, speaker assignments, translation and voice references.
3. Generate selected lines, listen for overlap, then export the required time range.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/estebanstifli/OpenDub/blob/main/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

- **Hardware:** Starting recommendations: four-core 64-bit CPU, 16 GB RAM, 20 GB free storage; 32 GB RAM and 12+ GB VRAM preferred. NVIDIA Q8 route recommends at least 8 GB VRAM; Apple 16 GB unified memory is a recommendation, not a tested lower bound.
- **Software:** Packaged .NET application, audio.cpp, llama.cpp, FFmpeg and VLC; no separate Python/compiler required for packages. NVIDIA drivers needed for CUDA.
- **Platforms:** Windows x64; Linux x64 targeting Ubuntu 24.04+; unsigned macOS 14+ Apple Silicon preview. CPU fallback, NVIDIA/Vulkan or Metal support varies by runtime.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT original code. IndexTTS has the bilibili Model Use License; Nemotron diarization uses OpenMDW-1.1. The optional BS-RoFormer ep368 checkpoint has unresolved commercial provenance. Bundled components include GPL/LGPL and proprietary CUDA terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT original code. IndexTTS has the bilibili Model Use License; Nemotron diarization uses OpenMDW-1.1. The optional BS-RoFormer ep368 checkpoint has unresolved commercial provenance. Bundled components include GPL/LGPL and proprietary CUDA terms.

### How it uses AI

A Mamba2-based neural painter predicts parametric brushstrokes from an image, then renders a stylized painting. [Source](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/multi_patch_inference_fast.py)

### Introduction

A Mamba2-based neural painter predicts parametric brushstrokes from an image, then renders a stylized painting. [Source 1](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/multi_patch_inference_fast.py)

[Source 1](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/multi_patch_inference_fast.py)

### Demo & examples

The README shows input/painting comparisons and links the paper; the inference route can save stroke parameters, intermediate patches and an animated GIF. These are developer examples, not our tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/multi_patch_inference_fast.py)

### Install

Clone the repository, install its requirements alongside the tested PyTorch version, and compile the selective-scan extension from the pinned VMamba revision in the README. Download a matching checkpoint directory containing config.yaml and last-model.pt. [Source 1](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/multi_patch_inference_fast.py)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the repository, install its requirements alongside the tested PyTorch version, and compile the selective-scan extension from the pinned VMamba revision in the README. Download a matching checkpoint directory containing config.yaml and last-model.pt.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter.git
```


```sh
cd MambaPainter
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python multi_patch_inference_fast.py MODEL_FOLDER INPUT.jpg --output output --image-size 512 --patch-size 64 --overlap-pixels 32 --stroke-padding 32 --save-all
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/STomoya/MambaPainter/blob/main/multi_patch_inference_fast.py)

1. Follow the pinned selective-scan build instructions before inference.
2. Place a downloaded configuration and checkpoint together; replace MODEL_FOLDER and INPUT.jpg.
3. Run a small image, inspect boundaries between patches, then inspect the optional stroke/timelapse outputs.

Separating a captured object from its scene, clipping a ground plane and rendering a cleaner turntable asset preview. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes examples and timing observations on an RTX 3060. Those timings apply to the author setup and are not an independent benchmark. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the linked video-to-splat/Beta-splatting environment, clone with submodules, install requirements and build the local CUDA extension. Prepare posed COLMAP images and a trained PLY. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the linked video-to-splat/Beta-splatting environment, clone with submodules, install requirements and build the local CUDA extension. Prepare posed COLMAP images and a trained PLY.

```sh
git clone --recursive https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment.git
```


```sh
cd splat-segment
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python setup.py build_ext --inplace
```


```sh
python segment.py --ply model.ply --dataset runs/car/dataset --out out/car
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

1. Provide a compatible PLY and dataset containing images and sparse/0 camera information.
2. Run segmentation and inspect masks and the selected splats.
3. Optionally use the documented ground-plane clipping and turntable examples.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The author reports an RTX 3060 example. No minimum VRAM, system RAM or total storage is specified; source builds require a compatible CUDA GPU/toolchain.
- **Software:** PyTorch/CUDA, compiled rasterization components, rembg and COLMAP-style camera data; follow the linked upstream reconstruction environment.
- **Platforms:** The CUDA source setup is documented. Native Apple GPU and a complete Windows/Linux support matrix are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 applies to the repository. gsplat/Beta-splatting and the selected rembg model must retain their own notices and model terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 applies to the repository. gsplat/Beta-splatting and the selected rembg model must retain their own notices and model terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The mask vote uses multiple views, with the documented default selecting support above 80% over at least five views. Removing splats does not reconstruct hidden interiors or guarantee a printable watertight mesh. [Source 1](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/neelgajera/splat-segment/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** An NVIDIA CUDA GPU is required for this backend; minimum VRAM, RAM and full storage are not documented. Pre-Ampere uses FP32 and may require more memory.
- **Software:** Python SDK 3.10–3.12 or Node SDK 20+. The backend uses PyTorch CUDA 12.8 for Blackwell and 12.6 for older NVIDIA cards. Provider adapters may pin additional SDK versions.
- **Platforms:** GPU service: Linux/WSL2 and NVIDIA. The documented backend does not support AMD or Apple GPUs. Browser viewing does not mean the GPU service runs natively on Mac.

### How it uses AI

A C/C++ inference engine encodes and decodes neural audio-codec representations, including Mimi, DAC and WavTokenizer, using GGUF model files. [Source](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A C/C++ inference engine encodes and decodes neural audio-codec representations, including Mimi, DAC and WavTokenizer, using GGUF model files. [Source 1](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Building local audio-token pipelines and comparing CPU/GPU codec reconstruction for creative audio applications. [Source 1](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README supplies model conversion, CLI decode and Python round-trip examples. It does not provide an independent listening-quality evaluation. [Source 1](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Build the CPU version with CMake or enable the documented backend for the target GPU. Convert a supported, separately obtained checkpoint to GGUF before decoding compatible token arrays. [Source 1](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build the CPU version with CMake or enable the documented backend for the target GPU. Convert a supported, separately obtained checkpoint to GGUF before decoding compatible token arrays.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp.git
```


```sh
cd codec.cpp
```


```sh
cmake -B build
```


```sh
cmake --build build -j
```


```sh
python scripts/convert-to-gguf.py --model-id kyutai/mimi --output mimi.gguf
```


```sh
./build/codec-cli decode --model mimi.gguf --codes input.npy --out output.wav
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

1. Build the default CPU route and obtain a supported model under its own terms.
2. Convert the model to GGUF, then provide compatible codec tokens or use the Python encode/decode API.
3. Listen to the reconstruction against the source before changing precision or quantization.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/mybigday/codec.cpp/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** CMake and a compatible C/C++ build environment; Python/model dependencies for conversion. pip install . builds the Python binding. Backend-specific SDKs are needed for acceleration.
- **Platforms:** CPU plus documented build switches for CUDA, Vulkan, Metal/macOS, SYCL, OpenCL, HIP/ROCm and other GGML backends. A listed switch is not evidence every model/backend combination was tested.

Choose the official package matching Windows, Linux or macOS. For source builds, use pixi and the documented CMake preset; the source path must not contain spaces. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the official package matching Windows, Linux or macOS. For source builds, use pixi and the documented CMake preset; the source path must not contain spaces. Configure Claude through the provided subscription/CLI route or an Anthropic API key.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/MSTECHtr/MSTech-AiCAD.git
```


```sh
cd MSTech-AiCAD
```


```sh
pixi install
```


```sh
pixi run cmake --preset conda-linux-release -B build/release -DENABLE_DEVELOPER_TESTS=OFF
```


```sh
pixi run cmake --build build/release
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/MSTECHtr/MSTech-AiCAD/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open a copy of a simple FreeCAD project and configure the assistant.
2. Specify dimensions and material assumptions, then inspect each generated feature and undo any unsuitable change.
3. Measure the final geometry and check exported parts in the target CAD/slicer workflow before fabrication.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/MSTECHtr/MSTech-AiCAD/blob/main/README.md)

A TypeScript composition engine lets AI coding agents author motion graphics, 3D scenes and video, inspect rendered frames and export through native WebGPU. ### How it uses AI

A macOS glyph renderer exposes MCP and CLI tools so an AI assistant can select palettes, character sets and animation settings and render variants. Its glyph matching and dithering are conventional algorithms, not a neural image model. [Source](https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A macOS glyph renderer exposes MCP and CLI tools so an AI assistant can select palettes, character sets and animation settings and render variants. Its glyph matching and dithering are conventional algorithms, not a neural image model. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Agent-assisted ASCII/glyph art, kinetic typography, lyric clips and stylized image/video variations with editable presets. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README presents a v0.2 showcase GIF and documented CLI/MCP examples. Developer rendering tests are evidence of implementation, not independent aesthetic evaluation. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the official macOS DMG or build the Rust/Tauri source. For AI control, configure an MCP client to run the app’s bundled executable with the mcp argument; the normal GUI works separately. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the official macOS DMG or build the Rust/Tauri source. For AI control, configure an MCP client to run the app’s bundled executable with the mcp argument; the normal GUI works separately.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio.git
```


```sh
cd glyph-grid-studio/src-tauri
```


```sh

```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Joona-t/glyph-grid-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Drop a permitted image into the GUI and choose a glyph set, palette and dithering mode.
2. For agent use, expose glyph_grid_catalog, glyph_grid_render and glyph_grid_batch through the documented MCP configuration.
3. Render a short GIF/MP4, inspect legibility and color, and save the preset before producing variants.

### How it uses AI

A model-driven CAD workbench turns reference images, dimensions and component requirements into editable Python/build123d BRep geometry with STEP, STL and 3MF exports. [Source](https://github.com/amagine-ai/Amagine3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/amagine-ai/Amagine3D/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Install Node.js 22.19+ and Python 3.10–3.13, clone the source and run npm install. ```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/amagine-ai/Amagine3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/amagine-ai/Amagine3D/blob/main/docs/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Configure the provider and open http://127.0.0.1:6160; API service defaults to port 6161.

3. Use the documented opt-in vision doctor if needed to verify image perception; inspect export readback and independently validate the design before fabrication.

## EdenLive

Video, animation & film · Interactive, immersive & live media · Games & production pipelines · WebXR, VR & AR

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-18; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Testing neural visual treatments for interactive scenes and game prototypes, or restyling a recorded proxy animation.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-18. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The project page and README show streaming examples. The separate LightX2V integration reports about 24.69 inference FPS on an RTX 5090 for its specific quantized 5B preset; this excludes loading, compilation, warmup and final MP4 saving. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/realtime/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES) [Source](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the documented Linux/CUDA environment and Python 3.10. Install the pinned PyTorch and real-time components, video dependencies and offline package as detailed in the README; download the model tree, then begin with a bundled sample using the 5B few-step profile. The optimized LightX2V route requires separate conversion and kernel setup. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/realtime/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES) [Source](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · documented:** The separate LightX2V project documents a working integration, hardware presets and measured throughput/memory. This review read its instructions but did not reproduce the results or independently assess visual fidelity. [Source](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** First-party and retained FlashDreams code are Apache-2.0; MIT notices remain for vendored GameBlocks/html-to-image components. Video models include Apache-2.0 and MIT components plus a DINOv3-licensed encoder. The optional FLUX first-frame workbench component is non-commercial/non-production and may require separate deployment authorization. Optional RTX post-processing uses proprietary NVIDIA bindings. Local or rented GPU compute and storage. Optional game-generation agents and restricted first-frame models add separate service and licensing conditions. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/realtime/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES) [Source](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The upstream program controls state; this is not evidence of a model reliably simulating game logic. Full weight download is about 210 GB. The optional prompt-to-game bridge uses a permissive agent execution mode and is outside the offline workflow reviewed here. Real-time throughput depends on exact kernels, quantization and hardware.

### How it uses AI

A neural renderer turns a proxy video, a target first frame and text direction into refined or stylized video. A separate game or scene engine maintains interaction state; the model renders its appearance. [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/realtime/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES) [Source](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)

### Install

Use the documented Linux/CUDA environment and Python 3.10. Install the pinned PyTorch and real-time components, video dependencies and offline package as detailed in the README; download the model tree, then begin with a bundled sample using the 5B few-step profile. The optimized LightX2V route requires separate conversion and kernel setup. [Source 1](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) [Source 3](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 4](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/realtime/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES) [Source 5](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented Linux/CUDA environment and Python 3.10. Install the pinned PyTorch and real-time components, video dependencies and offline package as detailed in the README; download the model tree, then begin with a bundled sample using the 5B few-step profile. The optimized LightX2V route requires separate conversion and kernel setup.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive.git EdenLive
```


```sh
cd EdenLive
```


```sh
conda create -n edenlive python=3.10 -y
```


```sh
conda activate edenlive
```


```sh
pip install torch==2.13.0 torchvision==0.28.0 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu130
```


```sh
pip install --no-deps -e ./realtime/flashdreams -e ./realtime/apps/cam2v -e ./realtime/integrations_v2/eden
```


```sh
pip install -r realtime/requirements.txt
```


```sh
pip install av==17.1.0 decord==0.6.0 pandas==2.3.3 pyarrow==25.0.1 imageio==2.37.4 imageio-ffmpeg==0.6.0
```


```sh
pip install --no-deps -e .
```


```sh
hf download VAST-AI/EdenLive --local-dir ./models/EdenLive
```


```sh
python -m edenlive.scripts.infer --config edenlive/configs/wan22_5b_720p_few_step.yaml
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. Default 5B bidirectional FA4 additionally needs SM90/BF16, flash-attn-4 4.0.0b31 and nvidia-cutlass-dsl 4.7.1. Node 22/Chromium are for game rendering, not proof of browser-only inference.

The upstream program controls state; this is not evidence of a model reliably simulating game logic. Full weight download is about 210 GB. The optional prompt-to-game bridge uses a permissive agent execution mode and is outside the offline workflow reviewed here. Real-time throughput depends on exact kernels, quantization and hardware. [Source 1](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) [Source 3](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 4](https://github.com/VAST-AI-Research/EdenLive/blob/main/realtime/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES) [Source 5](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/scripts/edenlive/README.md)

### How it uses AI

An AI planner translates natural-language part and assembly requests into a constrained program; CAD generators and mate solvers then create BRep solids and export STEP/GLB, drawings and bills of materials. [Source](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://huggingface.co/shib79/gemma-4-12b-constraintkit-GGUF)

### Introduction

An AI planner translates natural-language part and assembly requests into a constrained program; CAD generators and mate solvers then create BRep solids and export STEP/GLB, drawings and bills of materials. ### What it is good for

### Install

Clone the repository and configure a model provider privately. The documented primary route builds the cadkit Docker Compose service; an alternative Python install uses the all extra and starts Uvicorn on loopback port 8195. FreeCAD workbench installation is optional and uses a separate kernel environment. [Source 1](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shib79/gemma-4-12b-constraintkit-GGUF)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the repository and configure a model provider privately. The documented primary route builds the cadkit Docker Compose service; an alternative Python install uses the all extra and starts Uvicorn on loopback port 8195. FreeCAD workbench installation is optional and uses a separate kernel environment.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit.git
```


```sh
cd constraint-kit
```


```sh
docker compose -f cadkit/docker-compose.yaml up -d --build
```


```sh
curl http://127.0.0.1:8195/health
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shib79/gemma-4-12b-constraintkit-GGUF)

1. Configure the planner and an image-capable model for optional vision QA.
2. Submit a small, dimensioned assembly request to POST /build and inspect the returned parts, guessed parameters and files.
3. Review the geometric checks and exported STEP/GLB. Supply engineering boundary conditions explicitly for any physics analysis and validate the final part independently.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jajmangold/constraint-kit/blob/master/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shib79/gemma-4-12b-constraintkit-GGUF)

- **Hardware:** A hosted planner and local CAD compute are documented. The optional local model is a 9.8 GB GGUF download; this is file size, not a RAM/VRAM minimum. Minimum system RAM, GPU memory and full disk requirements are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+; Docker/Compose for the recommended service route. Kernel extras pin cadquery 2.7.0, build123d 0.10.0, ocp_gordon 0.1.18 and bd_warehouse 0.2.0. The base numpy/scipy install alone does not provide the AI CAD service. Hosted or local model endpoints are separate.
- **Platforms:** The pinned CAD kernel stack is documented as tested in the container; a complete host OS matrix is not supplied. The optional FreeCAD 1.0 workbench has a distinct Python/OCCT process and must not be treated as the same environment.

### How it uses AI

A multi-scale neural audio codec maps mono sound into hierarchical token sequences at different temporal resolutions and reconstructs audio from those codes. [Source](https://github.com/hubertsiuzdak/snac/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A multi-scale neural audio codec maps mono sound into hierarchical token sequences at different temporal resolutions and reconstructs audio from those codes. [Source 1](https://github.com/hubertsiuzdak/snac/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Experimenting with compact music/sound-effect representations and token-based audio-generation systems. [Source 1](https://github.com/hubertsiuzdak/snac/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the snac package in a compatible PyTorch environment, then load the separately provided checkpoint matching the intended sample rate. The README example explicitly moves the model and audio to CUDA.

```sh
pip install snac
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/hubertsiuzdak/snac/blob/main/README.md)

1. Select the 24 kHz speech model or the documented 32/44 kHz music/sound-effect model.
2. Provide actual mono audio with shape (batch, 1, time); the README random tensor is only a placeholder.
3. Call model.encode and model.decode, compare the reconstruction by listening, and preserve the token streams at their different lengths.

### How it uses AI

A full-duplex speech-text model and streaming neural audio codec support simultaneous listening and speaking through PyTorch, MLX or Rust implementations. [Source](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

### Introduction

A full-duplex speech-text model and streaming neural audio codec support simultaneous listening and speaking through PyTorch, MLX or Rust implementations. [Source 1](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

Choose the backend first. The PyTorch package starts a local model server; the MLX package supplies local Mac inference. Use a model repository matching the backend and quantization; the project recommends Python 3.12. [Source 1](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the backend first. The PyTorch package starts a local model server; the MLX package supplies local Mac inference. Use a model repository matching the backend and quantization; the project recommends Python 3.12.

```sh
pip install -U moshi
```


```sh
python -m moshi.server
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

1. For the PyTorch route, start the server and open localhost:8998 with microphone permission.
2. For a supported Mac, use the separate moshi_mlx package and its local_web entrypoint, or match the local CLI quantization flag to its model.
3. Test turn taking, echo and latency in a short session before incorporating it into an interactive artwork.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/kyutai-labs/moshi/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

- **Hardware:** The documented unquantized PyTorch route calls for a GPU with 24 GB memory. MLX has a tested M3 Mac example and quantized models, but no general minimum unified-memory/RAM or total storage requirement is given.
- **Software:** Python 3.10 minimum, with 3.12 recommended. Rust may be required for native bindings on other Python versions. Rust GPU builds require the appropriate CUDA/nvcc or Metal feature; CUDA Docker uses NVIDIA Container Toolkit.
- **Platforms:** MLX was tested upstream on a MacBook Pro M3; the Rust backend lists CUDA and macOS Metal. Windows is explicitly not officially supported. Backend requirements differ.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete root Apache license and separate MIT grant were read: Rust is Apache-2.0; Python and web-client code are MIT. The README declares model weights under CC-BY 4.0; attribution and individual model terms remain separate from software licenses.

### AI MoCap Data Collection System · Apache-2.0

Capturing body-motion datasets for later animation and interactive performance experiments.
MediaPipe camera pose estimation is combined with five BLE IMU sensor nodes to collect aligned movement data.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The documented Windows/Python route needs five ESP32 boards, five BMI160 sensors, a webcam and BLE. Saved data is resampled to 20 Hz; there is no shared hardware trigger, and clock drift can accumulate. Pose/model and firmware dependencies remain separate.

### Phrame · MIT

Turning a spoken session into changing visual material for an opt-in installation or studio display.

### AutoCAD MCP Pro · MIT

Agent-assisted drawing edits, layouts and measured geometry with a separately configured model client.
An MCP server lets an AI agent create and inspect CAD drawings through either Windows AutoCAD COM or a portable headless ezdxf engine.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT server code; live AutoCAD is a separate proprietary dependency. The headless engine handles DXF, lacks REGION/2D boolean fidelity, and needs rendering extras for pixels. Several feature tables are empty; takeoffs are estimates. Full setup, dependency/model terms and independent output checks remain pending.

### YominPost · MIT

Preparing drafts and storyboards for multiple channels from one piece of creative material.
A self-hosted social-content studio can use configured AI drivers to adapt source material into channel-specific copy, posters and short-video storyboards, with review and publishing connectors.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT code. The default offline template driver is deterministic, not an AI model; a configured CLI/API provider is required for model-generated copy. TikTok, Xiaohongshu, Threads and Pinterest connectors are simulated in the reviewed release. Social APIs, account approval, model costs and real connector reliability remain separate and unverified.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **Compositional Neural Painter** · needs-license-review: The complete software-license lookup returned 404; a published paper and repository do not establish an open-source grant. [Source](https://github.com/sjtuplayer/Compositional_Neural_Painter)
- **Stylized Neural Painting** · needs-license-review: The root CC0 license conflicts with the README statement that the work is licensed CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0. The scope needs clarification before open-source inclusion. [Source](https://github.com/jiupinjia/stylized-neural-painting/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/jiupinjia/stylized-neural-painting)
- **NeuralPreset** · needs-license-review: The paper describes a learned color-preset method, but a complete repository software license was not retrieved. The project-site page returned no substantive text. [Source](https://github.com/ZHKKKe/NeuralPreset) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13511)
- **NeRC** · excluded: The current repository tree contains only README and license, and the README says code will be released later. The caustics demo and dataset do not make an available application. [Source](https://github.com/JiaxiongQ/NeRC)
- **GitLab ComfyUI Foundation-1** · needs-license-review: The README describes a concrete music workflow but LICENSE returned 404. Its model terms are restricted and do not establish the wrapper software grant. [Source](https://gitlab.com/drbaph/ComfyUI-Foundation-1)
- **Open Airbrush Arm** · excluded: The current README explicitly describes early planning, and the tree contains no firmware, software or motion-capture implementation. A build plan is not yet an executable AI painting tool. [Source](https://github.com/Allowistic/open-airbrush-arm)
- **Uniscript MCP** · excluded: The repository contains documentation and images for a paid remote service, with no implementation source. Its license explicitly covers documentation and associated files; this does not establish an open-source carousel-generation service. [Source](https://github.com/sindebobel123/uniscript-mcp)
- **Avatar Motion Lab** · excluded: The current tree contains only the license file and no README or application implementation. Repository description alone does not establish an available AI motion tool. [Source](https://github.com/nanami9426/avatar-motion-lab)
- **Holo4D — model card with unavailable linked source** · needs-license-review: The current model card describes geometry-conditioned novel-view generation and claims released code. Its linked NIRVANALAN/Holo4D GitHub repository, README and license all returned 404 in this review; software availability and complete software license could not be established. Model metadata alone does not qualify an open-source tool. [Source](https://huggingface.co/yslan/Holo4D-2026)
- **OpenDesign — nonstandard license wording** · needs-license-review: GitHub reports Apache-2.0, but a complete word comparison found edits to the trademark exception in section 6 and the support/liability wording in section 9. Held for license clarification; the SPDX label alone does not establish an unmodified recognized software grant. [Source](https://github.com/nexu-io/open-design/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **3D Data Materials — simulated AI semantics kit** · excluded: The inspected open-vocabulary semantics kit explicitly does not run SAM, CLIP or DINOv2: it simulates model outputs and performs conventional 3D fusion. A linked Depth-Anything reconstruction implementation is a separate repository. This tutorial collection is not counted as an additional verified AI tool. [Source](https://github.com/florentpoux/3d-data-materials/tree/main/open-vocabulary-3d-semantics)
